Full current refresh: October 29, 2026 (late Pacific snapshot). 301 stable provenance records; separate pinned C++ and legacy baselines, current releases/branches and merged/pending states. Older snapshots remain historical evidence.

Evidence and scope
Records include overlap, alternatives, tools and experiments. They are not a roadmap or a defect count. Source presence, target builds, emulator observations and physical-console acceptance are different evidence levels. This documentation audit ran no console code.

Full current ecosystem refresh - October 29, 2026

Publication update - October 30, 2026: the All Games/Apps changes (#195 / #1163) have now merged in both editions. The CPP-only list-rebuild follow-up #201, Coverflow variants (#199 / #1167), Grid integration (#196 / #1164), silent in-game pause (#200 / #1168), config-import changes (#190 / #1159) and menu-freeze diagnostics (#191 / #1160) remain open at this publication check. The repository/ref/release inventory below remains the explicitly dated late-October-29 source snapshot.

This snapshot replaces the availability conclusions of the earlier October 29 reconciliation. It was collected late on October 29 in America/Los_Angeles; UTC evidence timestamps extend into October 30. Both sites retain the older reports and every stable anchor as dated history. Differences are opportunities for comparison, with shared work credited; they are not promised ports or a defect count.

Changes already merged in both editions

The October 29 hardening/read-only UDPBD/SMAP batch and per-mode positions are merged in both editions, as the published report already records. They are not still-open implementation work. Both now also contain:

Scope Current behaviour and limits
Beta in-game menu RIPPS2-derived OPL-core pause/resume, live cheats, exit and power off (RiptOPL #184, legacy #1155). Off by default; excluded from RA, IGS, freeze-report and EE SIO debug variants. Screenshot/Achievements pages, Neutrino and PS1 menus remain separate. Source documentation explicitly says hardware untested.
Display and themes Saved-video-mode boot (#170/#1141), SD/HD layout keys and aspect positioning (#181/#1152), fallback List/Coverflow/PSBBN/Grid for every theme (#183/#1154), priority restoration after hires (#180/#1151), 720p/1080i forced 16:9 (#189/#1158). The separate MC boot-audio sidecar remains RI45.
Input and artwork Missed pad reads retain held state; rumble alignment only when quiet (#188/#1157). PS1 cover/placeholder flicker was corrected (#174/#1144). These source changes do not identify the cause of every reported stall or establish console acceptance.
Runtime distribution RA kit descriptions link the exact ORBIT Neutrino RA runtime they ship, with a pin-watch workflow (#192/#1161 and #194/#1162). This is artifact pairing/maintenance, not a protocol/gameplay acceptance result.

The All Games/Apps changes (#195 / #1163) merged after the research snapshot and are present at the publication bases. The CPP-only list-rebuild follow-up #201, Coverflow variants (#199 / #1167), Grid integration (#196 / #1164), silent in-game pause (#200 / #1168), official-config import (#190 / #1159) and menu-freeze diagnostics (#191 / #1160) remain pending at publication. Their branch content is not folded into source availability. The in-game menu is credited to akilluminati47’s RIPPS2 and its ORBIT ancestry; multi-view/Grid remains credited to KrahJohlito and PSBBN Flow to darkladark.

RIPPS2 release, companion and development states

RIPPS2 main is 5ebb5e0b4cc0. The source patch queue is byte-for-byte unchanged from the earlier 75-patch build 124 reconstruction (git diff of elf/patches returned no delta). Main's 14 later commits concern release/docs, Media Studio, tests and screenshots; they do not add build 125 runtime code.

v1.2.4-beta/build124 is now released at b682a93c. GitHub sets prerelease=false while the product remains beta. This promotes builds122-124's launch transition, tar-merge and mode-position source from development status; it does not prove runtime or storage durability. RI42/RI46 are updated accordingly. RI47 remains an opt-in diagnostic mechanism even though its source is now tagged.

Media Studio is a distinct Windows companion release. It adds bounded AVI video conversion and packaging with ffmpeg/ffprobe alongside the existing audio/own-BIOS authoring toolchain. RI4/RI48 preserve companion scope; a media tool release is not a new game-loader version.

b125@524c2cef7c40 is an unreleased, diverged development branch, not current main. Source inspection adds RI49 (tar folders/member writes), RI50 (64KiB/64-slot packed recent-CFG LRU cache), and RI51 (video controls and HD-cover download choice). The branch also changes legacy sidebar defaults and corrects the built-in theme's omitted launch_disc key. Preserve staged-write, invalidation, memory-pressure and interruption questions; no upstream setup/build scripts or storage probes were executed.

YAOPL: shared fixes and a withdrawn policy

Canonical yaopl/yaopl master is 0889ac4d0095, ten commits beyond the earlier pin. Its rc2 release at ee9cb9fa remains a prerelease and predates master's later message-wrap work; the mutable nightly is separate.

YAOPL removed its DualShock launch reset in 7094cd25. Our bounded digital/unlocked handoff remains in both defaults. YA-06 is therefore a current policy divergence with an upstream withdrawal, not a claim of current upstream parity. No console evidence here selects the universally correct behaviour.

YA-19 finds existing local overlap: both ee_core padhook implementations preserve the hook flag during polling pauses, mark both callbacks and publish patched instructions through kernel-context cache flushes. YA-20 narrows the remaining comparison: local interactive message boxes already wrap, while YAOPL shares a helper across warnings, text screens, confirmations and removal prompts too. Width, height, UTF-8/CJK behaviour, truncation and each actual consumer need review before any port. Withdrawn YA-11 remapping stays deferred.

LUNA and runtime dependency pins

Wrapper c1205fbd now pins nhddl luna@1c97889d34ce. Its historical tracked frontend ref is legacy, not main, and is unchanged at4e8f4e6b. The earlier prose called that tracked ref main; this refresh corrects it. The five added frontend commits refine CubeGlass quick/game menus, padding/text fit, per-appearance effects colour and circular Space Flight stars; they do not add another VMC-storage backend.

Neutrino fff99d98, native OPL c533f939 and PSXCore 5d4de02f pins are unchanged. The previously inspected whole-card PS1/PS2 lifecycle, 4MiB direction-aware art reuse, paired long-argv and MMCE restore mechanisms remain differentiated comparisons. Existing local VMC Saves/creation, async caches and safe argv refusal overlap. Native/Neutrino/PSXCore flags cannot be transplanted without their paired runtimes. v3.1.3 release assets are not automatically this development wrapper.

Unchanged references OPL-JUMP, Blayr, xeRAbora client, PSXCore, PS2-Servers and other tracked refs/releases were refreshed. Prior source findings remain dated; no change is inferred from activity timestamps alone. Rolling release assets can change without a new tag name, so release metadata is retained separately.

Actionable comparisons and evidence limits

The immediate source comparisons are YA-20's remaining message consumers, RI42's archive merge/recovery contract, RI49/RI50's branch-only archive/cache ownership, and the scheduler/buffer invariants in T11. Whole-card VMC/PSU workflows remain separate from game-launch requirements. Hardware-sensitive controller and in-game-menu changes need affected-console evidence; this audit does not port them.

Disc ripping/CD-player/HDD partition writes remain declined local scope. Single-pass magnified HD and external-only runtime packaging remain optional design alternatives. Shared/imported fixes, intentional choices, tools and experiments stay in the register without being counted as defects.

Read the dated repository heads, releases, comparisons, complete manifests, dependency pins, pull requests, source review, per-ID dispositions and validation files beside this report. Local validation covers documents/generated indexes, anchors/resources, register/family totals and browser behaviour when available; its exact outcomes are recorded separately. No new PS2 build, emulator session, physical-console/gameplay, save durability, storage probe or performance acceptance was performed.

October 30 publication bases and PR-state update

Historical ecosystem reconciliation - October 29, 2026 (earlier snapshot)

Historical snapshot. Availability and release statements below refer to the earlier baseline. Use the full current refresh and the current register for today’s status.

The C++/ps2build RiptOPL line and the legacy C Open-PS2-Loader line are compared separately at pinned defaults. This reconciliation supersedes older availability wording while retaining every historical stable ID and its evidence. It is research and documentation, not a promise to port each difference.

Checked 141 repository identities / 138 canonical repositories. Of the 134 refs tracked on October 28: 10 changed, 124 unchanged, none unavailable. What is already present

Both editions have the merged RIPPS2 A–H integration: All Games, last-10 Recent, Last played sort, Random Game, hidden-game management, guarded game-file cleanup without APA deletion, JPG/JPEG and OPL Manager art aliases, Arabic/Persian shaping and CJK/Thai/Lao breaks, Drive Status/DIAG.TXT, VMC Saves browsing, glyph/weight/interface improvements, reset latches and the MMCE busy-seen wait. Grid and multi-view themes were added afterwards. Generic unified-library, recents, flat-grid and basic RTL rows are no longer described as absent.

The YAOPL hardening batches (RiptOPL #142, legacy #1114) merged history bounds, font terminators, cheat capacity, synchronous disc-key ownership and VMC fragment diagnostics. C++ already had the CNF equals guard; its regression and the legacy guard are present. Native OPL UDPBD/UDPFS IMG launches and the 16 KiB read cap are merged in #153 / #1123. Default still selects Neutrino on those sources. UDPFS Files games can also use the OPL core in both editions (RiptOPL #161, legacy #1134). Not tested on hardware.

Current POPS packages use Okeanos UDPFS Assault TEST with BIN/CUE support. This supersedes the earlier network-only 1.0.0 snapshot, not a restoration of the obsolete USB-first translator. Published driver-release metadata and packaging commits are distinct evidence from a passing network game/save test.

Batch merged after the first snapshot

RiptOPL #162 merged these PRs at 15:18 UTC on October 29, after the first snapshot of this audit; RiptOPL is reconciled at 2b948bc3. Legacy #1133 merged the same changes. Availability is determined from merged commits at the baseline, not an unchecked tracker. The two YAOPL planning PRs use different task numbers; this report uses the RiptOPL #131 / legacy #1105 scheme and spells out each mechanism.

Mechanism RiptOPL Legacy State

YA-18 read-only UDPBD shipped as the coordinated policy (write check, CFG fallback, no games.bin/folders/settings/VMC, hidden edit controls) together with refused-write-as-success in smap_udpbd, rather than either piece alone. The write check still uses the fixed name opl_write_test.tmp. Favorites/Recent/hidden-list writes were not re-audited here. No probe touched user storage.

YA-11 button remapping was removed upstream before release and remains deferred for an explicit product decision. UDPBD read-yield/SMAP-priority branches are A/B experiments, not demonstrated compatibility fixes.

RIPPS2: released 121, development 124

Released v1.2.1-beta / build 121 is pinned to 29e455b0. GitHub marks that release prerelease=false, while its own product name remains beta. Development main is b682a93cfffc, build 124. The review reconstructed 75 numbered patches on pinned legacy base 65d4b89; it did not execute upstream build/setup scripts or build a console ELF.

Builds 118–121 add ART Pack management, automatic picture cropping in 16:9, info/sidebar refinements, saved-mode boot-sky presentation and the MC boot-sound sidecar/copy offer. An early audio-start attempt was reverted after an upstream lab hang; the released ordering and later build-122 experiment are separated. Development-only additions are launch-disc spin/zoom (122), ART archive merge and recovery handling (123), and per-mode position profiles plus an opt-in thread-wait watchdog (124). The per-mode position profiles have since been ported (RI44, RiptOPL #159 / legacy #1131). None is promoted to a console compatibility fix.

New records RI41–RI47 preserve this released/development split. Existing RI1/RI5/RI19/RI21/RI23/RI35/RI36/RI38 are narrowed around the portions already ported. Disc ripping and HDD partition writes remain declined local scope; the magnified single-pass HD renderer and external-only runtime packaging remain optional design alternatives.

LUNA: follow the wrapper pins

LUNA wrapper b65ec63a pins frontend luna@f640a4e8cfd4, Neutrino fff99d98, native OPL c533f939 and unchanged PSXCore 5d4de02f. The previously tracked frontend main ref is unchanged; reviewing only that ref would miss 17 frontend commits. Published v3.1.3 and its separately pinned hotfix workflow are not automatically the same artifact as this development wrapper.

The actual source now unifies PS1/PS2 whole-card management with raw-format validation, staged byte-verified copies, backup/replace/recover gates and independent PS1 slots. Local creation/assignment and the merged Saves browser overlap but do not provide that entire lifecycle (LU13/LU33/LU35/LU44). Source recovery paths are not proof of power-loss durability.

Additional source comparisons are direction-aware 4 MiB decoded-cover reuse (LU41), paired resident long-argv transport (LU42), and opt-in physical-MMCE switching with session-slot restore in both paired runtimes (LU43). Local async art caching, safe kernel argument refusal, GameID and native IGR already overlap. Do not transplant LUNA loader flags or advertise larger argv without its matching runtime. Crystal menus, camera/background changes and reverted font-spacing work are presentation history, not a missing generic theme engine.

OPL-JUMP and new forks

OPL-JUMP 414674c2 contributes narrower optional loose-shortcut discovery and filename-to-shelf classification (JUMP1/JUMP2). Local tagged Apps, PS1 launching and All Games already exist. The earlier real-function classifier probe remains historical evidence, not a test rerun here: its prefix rule misclassifies a PS2-looking SLUS name and omits SCPS. Never infer launch behavior from shelf membership.

Blayr OPL Custom 7959031a is seven commits ahead of current upstream b3a21997. Source comparison adds BL1 (device-local/shared MC Favorites policy) and BL2 (bounded idle-queue COV/ICO neighbour prefetch). Local Favorites identity/failure handling and art workers overlap. The author distinguishes an older real-PS2 prefetch trial from newer Favorites/UI emulator checks; neither is acceptance for these editions.

The latest 30 upstream OPL forks and latest 30 RiptOPL forks were enumerated. Newly found taisonboxing3-code matches upstream; Diegoprofeta matches the RiptOPL baseline; 1247847495 retains ancestor 6edea174. Blayr is the only new default branch with independent source changes in this bounded discovery. This does not enumerate all historical/private forks or analyze every advertised branch.

Evidence files and review limits

  • Every tracked/default ref and advertised branch/tag
  • Current release metadata, limits and redirects
  • Tracked comparison summaries and complete local file manifests for capped comparisons
  • Wrapper/runtime pins and reconstruction
  • PR snapshot with exact heads and new-fork comparisons

GitHub compares cap their file list at 300; some commit lists also exceed 100. Local Git manifests recover complete path counts for RIPPS2 (569), legacy (320), Launchstation (371), Odyssey (328) and PS2SP (3,512). A complete path inventory is not a claim to have audited every changed function. RIPPS2 patch mechanisms and LUNA pinned frontend/runtime deltas received targeted source review; companion/catalogue changes received narrower review as stated.

An unchanged tracked branch preserves the earlier source evidence, not hardware acceptance and not proof that every other branch/tag is unchanged. Every carried-forward register row says so. Both editions have independent baseline SHAs; later merges require a new reconciliation. No target build, emulator/game run, physical console run, storage probe, gameplay/save-durability check or throughput benchmark was performed for this documentation update.

Maintenance contract

The current register, metrics, consolidated families and search index are regenerated from this dated disposition set. Historical October 25/28/29-focused findings retain their URLs and anchors but are labelled historical. Stable IDs are never reassigned. Keep source date, target baseline, release/development stage, pending PR heads and evidence level with every future status change. Do not update only a new prose section while leaving the filterable register stale.

Historical focused refresh — October 29, 2026: YAOPL and OPL-JUMP

Historical snapshot. Current availability is reconciled in the October 29 ecosystem audit and the searchable register; later merged fixes are not missing merely because this dated section says so.

Pinned source audit · Structured findings and host-probe results. Four scoped mechanism/workflow differences follow; historical register totals below remain dated snapshots, not a current total. This section supersedes older recommendations for YA-11.

This is a source comparison of newly changed YAOPL code and OPL-JUMP, not a new ecosystem-wide sweep. It supplements the dated October 25/28 register without rewriting its historical counts or stable IDs. No console/game testing was performed. The small JUMP host probe below checks classification only.

Pinned revisions and provenance

Project Compared revision
YAOPL, by belomaxorka / Roman Kelesidis fc5426469c04, changes since previously reviewed b0f8c2ecaa11
OPL-JUMP, by juankyt505 414674c24485; implementation published in 1e98bbf
RiptOPL C++ / ps2build rebuild/main@b4c23bdac8f983a4b4e3456d4036b8645cf95842
Open-PS2-Loader legacy C rebuild/main@29826585edd960b312ba58d2f6987ac345eae3a9

OPL-JUMP identifies its upstream base as OPL revision 2267, 4cc74baabb73be9ca43f817474b321cd70fbcc2d. Its README reports USB/dual USB, traditional HDD, SMB1 and MX4SIO tests by its author; those reports were not reproduced here. Preserve its AFL-3.0 attribution and original OPL notices in any future port.

New or refined differences in both editions

ID Classification Difference and evidence Recommended next step
YA-17 Missing shutdown mechanism; console impact unverified YAOPL e72c9f48 stops operational OHCI after unmount/flush and before the IGR IOP reset, reusing its UDPBD startup helper. Both our modules/iopcore/cdvdman/cdvdman.c::oplShutdown() paths drain VMC, lock and unmount, but lack this USB-host stop. Upstream describes an owner's DS3 USB PADEMU IGR hang; its post-fix console verification was still pending. Investigate/adapt the lifetime fix, retaining our stronger VMC drain, shutdown ordering, USB storage and RetroAchievements hooks. Verify no-transfer, active USB/BT PADEMU, USB storage, power-off, repeated IGR and timeout cases before claiming a hang fixed.
YA-18 Missing coordinated read-only UDPBD policy YAOPL's 093b541b, 7781959c, 11037583, c92f681d, afe59be2, 7c375a09 and 760d7e3c detect discarded writes, redirect per-game CFG, suppress automatic writes, block edits and disable VMC on a read-only server. Neither edition has the corresponding per-device read-only flag/policy. Design capability detection and audit every write path, including our additional Favorites/Recent/hidden/Apps/config/cache operations. Keep gameplay readable and saves on writable media; preserve stored VMC preferences while preventing use on read-only backing. Do not copy the probing/driver patch mechanically.
JUMP1 Partial overlap; automatic categorization missing OPL-JUMP src/appsupport.c classifies boot-name prefixes into Apps vs PS1 shortcut filters. Our Apps library already filters via the explicit [PS1] title tag, and separately recognizes POPSTARTER forms for launch preparation. Automatic filename-to-shelf classification is the narrower difference. Optional display-only classification with explicit category override, documented regional IDs, extension/path validation and false-positive tests. Never use guessed shelf membership to select a launch core, bypass REBOOTIOP prompts, or turn an ordinary ELF into POPSTARTER. Preserve current tagged apps, IDs, favorites and theme families.
JUMP2 Partial overlap; loose-shortcut discovery missing OPL-JUMP src/menusys.c scans mass0..4 roots/POPS/APPS, an HDD APPS location and SMB root/POPS/APPS into its JUMP portal. Our unified All Games/PS1 views already aggregate sources, but Apps discovery reads conf_apps.cfg and per-app title.cfg, rather than indexing those loose root/POPS shortcut ELFs. Consider opt-in loose-shortcut discovery through the existing library/worker, not a duplicate hard-coded JUMP UI. Bound scanning, deduplicate by source/path, preserve arguments and identity, handle removal/reconnect and avoid synchronous filesystem work during rendering. Do not copy fixed five-slot assumptions or overwrite existing configured entries.

YA-18 port boundary: detection is a design task

YAOPL writes, reads and removes a 512-byte timer-seeded yaopl_write_test.tmp at the UDPBD root. Its separate df65546e patch treats an explicit refused write as completed so FatFs can continue reading; it relies on the subsequent policy suppressing writes. This is not a general-purpose write-success correction. Importing that patch alone could conceal lost writes. A fixed filename opened with truncation also requires collision/ownership review before adoption. Prefer negotiated server capabilities where available; otherwise specify safe probe ownership, cache bypass, error/permission vs read-only distinction and cleanup. No probe was run on user storage in this audit.

Acceptance must include servers that persist, discard, explicitly reject or time out writes; stale cached/probe data; reconnects; unavailable writable CFG locations; game and Apps rename/delete; global settings; games.bin and our other automatic writes; both VMC slots, autolaunch, physical-card fallback and Neutrino/native differences. Track shared CFG identity and avoid edits on writable copies silently affecting read-only launches.

JUMP classification limitations verified on the host

A host C harness compiled OPL-JUMP's actual appIsPS1Launcher() function, with only the app_info_t.boot container stubbed:

Boot name JUMP classifier
XX.Game.ELF, SB.Game.ELF PS1 shortcut
SLUS_203.12.PS2.ELF PS1 shortcut (prefix alone cannot establish platform)
SCPS_100.00.PS1.ELF Ordinary app (regional prefix not in the four-ID-prefix list)
Tool.ELF Ordinary app
XX.not_an_elf PS1 shortcut (this Apps predicate does not enforce the extension)

The loose-file scanner's separate ps1testIsElf() does check .ELF; do not generalize the Apps predicate's extension limitation to every JUMP code path. The code checks prefix families, not a fully validated PS1 executable ID. These are categorization observations, not launch or hardware results.

Withdrawn and experimental work — do not count as new fixes

  • YA-11 button remap was removed upstream by 6faa44e4. It reverts the initial feature, settings and IGR correction because the owner no longer wants the complexity; upstream says it never shipped in a release. Keep our previous task ID/provenance, but defer it for an explicit local product decision rather than treating an active YAOPL feature as missing. DS34 USB ownership fixes remain separate (YA-07).
  • New test/udpbd-read-yield, test/udpbd-read-yield-nopadreset, test/udpbd-smap-prio and test/udpbd-smap-prio-nopadreset branches are A/B experiments. The default branch does not contain them. Add them as investigation leads under YA-08, not established compatibility wins or mandatory imports.
  • OPL-JUMP's separate portal, L3 button, device-group headings, blue cover frame, Spanish strings and author hardware-test list are presentation choices or evidence context. Our existing unified library, filters, PS1 art and language support overlap these; no separate missing PS1 engine, SMB backend, multi-USB support or generic L3 feature is established.

Existing implementation work remains separate

The recent YA-01/04/05/10/15/16 fixes have independent PRs, not guaranteed default-branch availability. Consult the edition's YAOPL tracking PR (RiptOPL #131 / legacy #1105) and linked PR status. This audit does not mark them merged or hardware-verified and does not re-count them as new October 29 gaps.

RIPPS2 audit and port (findings by akilluminati47)

The findings are akilluminati47's, read from RIPPS2's source and changelog and checked against our code. RIPPS2 is a patch stack on the legacy C tree, so each item is a port by hand, credited to akilluminati47 in its commits. Every port lands in both RiptOPL and Open-PS2-Loader; an item that cannot be ported to one of them gets an issue instead.

Historical source sweep — October 25, 2026

Fork gap refresh — October 25, 2026

Baseline RiptOPL 55261de1381ae70ecfdfb2054f9cafbcfcd586cf. Rechecked 128 tracked repository identities / 126 canonical repositories: seven changed, 121 unchanged, zero unavailable. All 128 release feeds were checked. Six newly created upstream forks were also compared; this is a bounded discovery sweep, not a claim to enumerate every PS2 project.

The register now contains 258 stable records and 118 capability/design families. New records: RI34, RI35, RI36, RI37, RI38, RI39, RI40, YA1, NV1, LU40. Five new families cover runtime negotiation, MMCE firmware coordination, artwork interoperability, recent-library navigation and experimental in-game volume. Correctness investigations remain separate from feature families. Earlier IDs, historical dossiers and H11's closed source-parity disposition are preserved.

Changed tracked projects

Project Assessment
RIPPS2 Released Build 114 and Build 115 source reviewed separately; see the complete deep dive
LUNA Wrapper gitlinks now pin frontend df394982 and PSXCore 5d4de02; tolerant/partial scan recovery, separately pinned v3.1.3 hotfix workflow
PSXCore Removes normal startup diagnostics and increases native USB CD RPC thread stack to 16 KiB; backend-specific correctness work, not a universal PS1 fix
Launchstation PR 206 merged; final history restores R3Z ordinary transfer/overwrite behavior. Earlier intermediate copy-safety work is not proof of the final implementation; no new console claim
DKWDRV README announces 1.7.6u timing rewrite and explicitly anticipates random crashes/hangs. Release-description evidence; no implementation delta audited from this README-only compare
OdysseyLauncher 0.19.0 console-model/theme expansion; desktop frontend scope
PS2SP 43 catalogue/discovery/deployment commits; API file-list cap means catalogue discovery is not exhaustive feature review

Newly discovered forks

YAOPL adds substantial hardening and MMCE work. Its 99-commit comparison includes merge commits and shared RiptOPL-derived changes; review by mechanism. The OPL-volume fork adds volume RPC and framebuffer overlay source but labels it uncompiled. Four other new default branches equal upstream, including experiment-native-chd: no CHD implementation is established there.

Evidence and remaining work

Every ref, tracked comparisons, new-fork comparisons, release feeds, RIPPS2 deep dive, every stable ID, deduplicated families, host checks and validation.

Unchanged source heads retain the prior source review. The sweep advertises branches/tags but does not analyze every branch. API comparisons can cap files at 300. No physical PS2 run, gameplay compatibility, save durability or throughput benchmark was performed. No foreign runtime/driver was ported.

October 19, 2026 — focused delta audit

This pass compares selected active projects against RiptOPL rebuild/main@2eb0d376 (checked October 19 UTC). It is not a full rescan of every project in the A–Z directory. Git history/code and README capabilities are separate from physical console test evidence.

ResultIDsEvidence and interpretation
No longer missingCF1–CF3RiptOPL #955 integrates PC CARD CF menu/in-game paths. Hardware acceptance is still pending. Companion file-manager work CF4 is separate.
No longer missingU1Per-game parental lock is implemented and host-tested, with real-console UI validation remaining.
New source deltasLU12–LU14LUNA gained 40 commits since the last pinned comparison, including PS1 disc-set/swap control, save-card tools and expanded file management.
New public projectPX1–PX3PSXCore is an inspectable experimental POPS runtime adapter; its standalone tester disables IGR and disc switching, unlike LUNA's separately configured integration. PS2 hardware validation is pending.

No new distinct capability foundExisting O / OH / OROPLattice changed SMB setup documentation only; OPHTML is at the earlier source head; ORBIT improved covers and launch/network behavior without a confirmed new feature ID.
Updated fork candidateRI5RIPPS2 Build 104 has stable English-hash keyed language packs and layered glyph fallback, unlike RiptOPL's positional format. The implementation exists in main; this build was not yet released at audit time. Builds 105–107 are proposals, not current gaps.
New upstream fix to verifyRA / no counted new IDxeRAbora alpha.18 fixed corruption of memory-address watch lists for very large achievement sets (hardware-reported 2,679-address example) and a hardcore-status API bug. RiptOPL bundles and documents alpha.17; RiptOPL's chunk validation tests need a size-specific parity check before declaring either bug missing. Alpha.18's UI list/grid, rich presence and unlock-overlay toggle are PC-client options rather than inherently OPL-core gaps. The upstream RA fork removed PS2RD cheats; do not infer RiptOPL must remove them.
Deeper PS1 runtime auditPX4–PX10, LU15The earlier audit underclassified PSXCore runtime components. This pass separates FMV, audio, CPU, patch safety, PS1 VMC and disc-identity behaviors from baseline PS1 launching, which RiptOPL already supports. Source differences do not prove superior game compatibility. LUNA's two latest commits after the prior audit changed README text only.

Newly discovered but not counted as additional gaps: LUNA's OrbitPS2 Manager fork specializes an existing OM-series tool; LUNA's OPL fork implements a second PS2 runtime already selectable in RiptOPL. EonPS2 has no confirmed independent change in this pass; SMB4PS2 is a Samba server wrapper, not a new console protocol.

OPLattice

coffeedevsolutions/OPLattice — nine patches to OPL's theme engine and menus, a sidebar interface, themes and PC tooling.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
O1Grid game listA tile layout for the game list (columns, cell_width, gap, frame) with two-axis navigation.patch 01
O2GameImage offset for non-selected gamesAn image element can draw the game a set number of places down the page, which makes hero and filmstrip layouts possible.patch 01
O3Recently played listThe last 8 games launched, newest first, shown with RecentImage and RecentText elements. RiptOPL remembers only the last one.patch 02
O4Sort modesA to Z, Z to A, most recent first, or as scanned. OPLattice cycles them with R3, which RiptOPL uses for Favorites.patch 02
O5MenuTabs device tab stripEvery device drawn at once with the active one highlighted, plus per-device label overrides.patch 03
O6GameCountText prefix and suffixThemes set their own wording around the game count.patch 03
O7x_scaled widescreen alignmentElements keep their position relative to a centred layout when widescreen is on.patch 03
O8VRAM texture budget clamp FixCaps texture size to the video memory left after the framebuffers, so an oversized theme image is rejected instead of risking a hang in hi-res modes.patch 07
O9Play statsA launch count and playtime for each game.patch 08
O10SHELF sidebar interfaceAn opt-in sidebar with Home, Library, Apps and Settings pages. It opens with L3, which RiptOPL uses to switch between PS2 and PS1 views.patch 09

OPHTML

coffeedevsolutions/OPHTML by coffeedevsolutions (Blake Coffee) — an HTML/CSS declarative UI compiler and zero-allocation C99 GS runtime (OPHTML / ps2ui), by the creator of OPLattice. While the repository also contains a standalone game launcher (console/ophtml.elf) booting ISOs via Neutrino and an SD2PSX memory-card loader (channel6), those launcher features overlap capabilities already native to RiptOPL (see callout below). Audited against main@03a5d56 (post-v0.10.0 source, format v7). The latest published release remains v0.10.0; the reusable console core, settings resolver and memory-card state changes are source additions planned for 0.11.0.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
OH1Declarative HTML/CSS flexbox compilation to zero-allocation C99 GS command listCompiles modern CSS flexbox layouts (direction, wrap, alignment, justification, gap, percentage sizing, border radius) and dynamic UTF-8 text slots with multibyte truncation guards on the PC into a static .uib binary command list. The C99 runtime executes inside a pre-computed static arena (~7–8 KiB for an entire 6-screen app) and never calls malloc, measures text, or lays out elements on the PS2.@ophtml/layout
runtime/ps2ui.c
OH2Pre-resolved pairwise HarfBuzz font kerning on EEMeasures kerning pairs at compile time via HarfBuzz and pre-resolves pairwise advance adjustments to exact pixel values per font size into compact tables. The C99 pen evaluates pairwise kerning in real-time on the EE with zero floating-point division or font-scaling overhead.ps2ui_bake/fontgen.py
ps2ui.c kerning
OH3Instant 1-byte palette theme switching without VRAM re-uploadMultiple themes (such as light and dark modes) share the same geometry, textures, and fonts in a single blob, differing only by a row in the tint table. Calling ps2ui_theme_set() updates a context pointer instantly with 0 bytes of VRAM transfer, requiring no asset reloading or GS re-initialization.ps2ui.h
ps2ui.c
OH4Runtime 2D coordinate offsets for motion and expanding layoutsps2ui_offset_set(ctx, dx, dy) offsets all primitive and texture coordinates at render time, allowing smooth programmatic translations, sliding sidebars (such as expanding navigation rails), carousels, and parallax scrolling layers on static baked geometry without re-baking.ps2ui.c offset
BACKLOG F27
OH5Runtime focus-subtree visibility masking with auto-bypassing navigationA 256-bit bitmask in ps2ui_visible_set() suppresses rendering of entire focusable subtrees (including backgrounds and borders) for empty or inactive elements. Crucially, ps2ui_move() automatically routes gamepad focus past hidden nodes rather than trapping navigation on invisible elements.ps2ui.c visibility
BACKLOG F21
OH6Automated CRT overscan safe area and multi-layer alpha contrast linter ToolBuild-time static analysis tool enforcing NTSC/PAL CRT action-safe and title-safe margins. Evaluates text contrast across translucent alpha scrims by compositing against worst-case black and white background scenes underneath, and validates seated-distance legibility (11px/14px thresholds based on visual arcminutes).layout/src/lint.js
findings.md (F-046)

OH8Static VRAM budgeting and layout compiler ToolModels GS VRAM allocation at compile time, tracking 8 KiB page boundaries and 256 B block alignment groups for framebuffers, Z-buffers, CLUT palettes, and texture pages. Prevents console crashes and hangs by refusing over-budget themes at bake time with an itemized memory breakdown.ps2ui_bake/vram.py

OH9Headless Play! emulator CI testbed with input scripting and frame diffs ToolContinuous integration harness that builds the console ELF, boots it headlessly in the Play! emulator under xvfb, injects scripted gamepad inputs (Down, R1, L1), captures GS framebuffers, and asserts pixel-level image diffs against Python reference renders..github/workflows/hw.yml

OH10Fuzzed .uib loader with EE bus-alignment and malformed blob hardening FixRuntime loader hardened with LibFuzzer and ASan/UBSan against corrupted or malicious blobs. Enforces strict 4-byte struct alignment and 16-byte glyph/kern table alignment (PS2UI_ERR_ALIGN) to protect against unaligned EE memory access bus errors when reading untrusted themes from storage.fuzz_load.c

OH11Hard compiler safety limits for untrusted themes FixCompile-time safety ceilings that reject untrusted or maliciously crafted themes exceeding safe hardware parameters: canvas max 2048px (hardware scanout ceiling), max 10,000 DOM elements, max 64 nesting levels, and max 32 megapixel source images.caps.py
limits.js
OH12Reusable console-launcher core archiveExports libophtml-console.a and an umbrella header for third-party launchers to reuse scanning, storage initialization, Neutrino argument construction, settings resolution and launch handoff while supplying their own main, UI and IOP module table. RiptOPL has internal equivalents but does not publish this kind of independently linkable launcher SDK. This is a developer integration gap, not additional game compatibility.Public core header
Out-of-tree consumer
What did not become a gap: The console launcher & runtime overlap
OPHTML also contains a standalone console game launcher (console/ophtml.elf) and sample environments (opl-env, channel6), its core game-loading and management capabilities directly overlap existing RiptOPL infrastructure and are not counted as gaps:
  • Neutrino No-IOP-Reset launch handoff: OPHTML launches Neutrino using -qb, -bsd=, and -dvd= through LoadELFFromFileWithPartitionNoReset. RiptOPL already has full native No-IOP-Reset handoff (src/elfldr_noreset.c), copying the child loader into BIOS-unused memory at 0x84000 and passing target paths and arguments to ExecPS2 so Neutrino can read its config and ISO without resetting OPL's live storage mounts.
  • In-memory ISO9660 volume & SYSTEM.CNF title ID extraction: OPHTML traverses sector 16 PVD records and parses SYSTEM.CNF in memory. RiptOPL already performs sector 16 PVD root-directory traversal in memory to extract BOOT2 game IDs and creation timestamps (src/retrogem.c and src/ps2cnf.c).
  • Dynamic cover art streaming & VRAM texture caching: OPHTML's streaming texture slots overlap RiptOPL's dedicated async texture worker (src/texcache.c), which provides lookahead caching, multi-epoch invalidation, chunked staging, memory clamps, and SIO2 controller-bus read throttling.
  • Virtualized list windowing & selection binding: OPHTML's ps2ui_list windowing and selection clamping are standard OPL core features in RiptOPL's item and GUI management.
  • Multi-device & SD2PSX/MMCE support: USB, internal HDD, MX4SIO, and MMCE channel support are already implemented in RiptOPL's storage drivers and RetroGEM/SD2PSX integration.
Validation status and emulator testing
OPHTML v0.10.0's ophtml.elf launcher has booted and proven its D-pad navigation and list virtualisation inside the Play! emulator in CI (via automated pad input injection and frame diffing). However, because Play! does not emulate USB, HDD or memory card controllers, physical-console bench cases C1–C8 remain formally [open] pending physical-console reports. Conversely, its underlying ps2ui GS rasterization runtime has undergone extensive physical PS2 hardware validation across multiple test sessions (documented in docs/bench-phase1.md and docs/bench-phase2.md).

The new OPL compatibility resolver maps only verified modes 2, 3 and 5; GSM and VMC CFG mapping remain unfinished. Last-played restoration overlaps RiptOPL. The launch breadcrumb records an attempted handoff but is not a proven hang detector or retry UI; it is not counted as one.

PS2-Launcher v5.0 overlap
v5.0's Files tab (browse devices, copy, paste and delete files, launch games and ELFs) overlaps B1, and its Xbox 360 support overlaps MP1; those receive no new P-series IDs. Already in RiptOPL: MMCE storage, the full in-game resolution list (RiptOPL also has 720p, 1080i and 1080p), the IGR Path setting, per-game OSD language, and multitap emulation for extra players (set up in Controller Emulation rather than assigned automatically). Its new SMB IP options are not itemised; RiptOPL already offers DHCP or a static address with gateway and DNS, and reaches the server by IP or name. The faster Apps tab is a performance change, not a capability.

ORBIT

danielnuld/ps2-launcher by danielnuld — a standalone PS2 launcher built around Neutrino. Audited against d015baf7 and RiptOPL rebuild/main@dc6206f6. Re-checked on 2026-10-06 against v1.0.2: v1.0.1 and v1.0.2 are fixes (a 1024-game limit, cover loading, DNS with DHCP, and MemCard PRO 2 game IDs on every launch, which RiptOPL already sends), so they add no entries. OR1 and OR2 have since shipped in RiptOPL (see Notes). These are capability differences, not claims that every game or storage device has passed on a console.

ORBIT overlap and validation
ORBIT's on-console HTTPS cover fetch overlaps P2; its combined-source library and animated launch screen overlap P3 and P5; and its resumable Neutrino in-game menu overlaps the capability tracked in T3 and LU8. ORBIT's phase-13 report records a menu pass in one game at 480p, with other video modes and later core builds needing separate checks. Those overlaps receive no new OR-series IDs. ORBIT's standard-source and Neutrino per-game options, PS1/Apps paths and two interface languages also have RiptOPL counterparts.

Titan validation and overlap notes
Titan distinguishes source/host-contract completion from physical-PS2 validation. In particular, Smart IGR, the modern-controller paths and Store/Download path are kept above because this page audits implemented code capabilities, but Titan's own hardware/target-pending labels are preserved rather than treating them as proven compatibility.

Several other Titan capabilities are real but do not get extra T-series IDs because the same RiptOPL gap is already represented elsewhere on this page: direct cover/background fetching overlaps P2, its native wLaunchELF work overlaps B1, Project NES overlaps R1, its launch transition overlaps P5, and wired Xbox USB overlaps P1. Titan's Neutrino/UL launching, VMC support and ordinary PS2RD cheats also have RiptOPL equivalents and are not gaps by themselves.

xeRAbora

RiptOPL's RA build now follows upstream xeRAbora alpha.15. PR #881 implemented the larger watch sets and cadence (retired H2) and alignment-safe bounded reads (retired H3). H1 pointer chains were already present. H1–H3 reflect earlier parity; H4–H10 now record alpha.18 and companion differences in the inclusive inventory. RA with Neutrino remains the separate OR3 capability. Successful host tests and target builds do not establish console interoperability. See RetroAchievements for the paired client and validation scope.

NHDDL

pcm720/nhddl — a deliberately small Neutrino front-end whose README explicitly keeps OPL-only features such as PADEMU, IGR and cheats out of scope unless Neutrino itself provides them. Audited against the released v1.2.2 code and documentation. The core features most likely to look unique at first glance are already present in RiptOPL: PixelFX/RetroGEM visual Game ID signalling, per-title SD2PSX/MemCard PRO2 card switching, cover art, persistent title caching, remembered last-played selection, global and per-game Neutrino arguments, VMC handoff and the principal Neutrino storage backends. The two remaining differences below are launcher/network conveniences rather than missing game-source support.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
N1Generic forward-boot CLI OptionalLaunching NHDDL with -mode=<type> and -dvd=<path> bypasses its UI and hands the target straight to Neutrino; -noinit can reuse an environment whose modules are already loaded. RiptOPL has positional HDD/BDM autolaunch entry points, but not an equivalent documented generic backend/path command line.Forward Boot Mode
N2Reuse SYS-CONF/IPCONFIG.DAT for UDPFS IP OptionalWhen no explicit udpfs_ip is supplied, NHDDL can obtain the PS2 network address from the wLaunchELF/uLaunchELF-style SYS-CONF/IPCONFIG.DAT. RiptOPL's UDPFS path currently uses its own Network settings and the active Neutrino TOML instead of inheriting that shared file.UDPFS documentation
IPCONFIG.DAT fix
What did not become a gap
NHDDL's visual Game ID output and SD2PSX/MemCard PRO2 per-title switching both have direct RiptOPL equivalents. RiptOPL also already remembers the last-played title, caches parsed ISO metadata, supports global and per-game Neutrino arguments, and can hand VMCs to Neutrino. NHDDL's backend-forcing ELF filename suffixes are a different configuration interface for selecting a device mode, not a separate game-loading capability, so they are not counted independently.

LUNA

The detailed text in this earlier source section is retained as dated evidence. Use the full current refresh and current per-ID register above for updated pins, merged features and development status.

LUNA by darkladark is a visual NHDDL/Neutrino-derived loader. Earlier comparison: 475f7b25, pinning frontend e9554ce6 and runtime 02cbf4f2. These are separate repositories; implementation links below point to their exact gitlink revisions. The current views are List, Collections, 3D, Orbit and experimental Scroll. Constellation (LU1) and the old flat Grid (LU3) are historical, not current capabilities.

Collection display overlap: RiptOPL #977 adapts the Collection display from #733 as the native PSBBN Flow theme. It preserves R3 Favorites and uses the existing menu and artwork cache. This perspective fan does not implement LUNA's orbital ring or projected 3D grid listed below.

LU5Per-drive Favorites that travel with the game HDD OptionalLUNA resolves Favorites against the selected title's metadata device and stores them under that drive's /LUNA/favorites.txt; swapping game HDDs therefore swaps the Favorites set with the library. RiptOPL deliberately keeps one cross-device favourites.bin beside its shared master configuration, so this is a different persistence model rather than missing Favorites functionality.favorites.c

LU6Staged temp-file writes for key persistent library state FixLUNA writes a complete temporary file before replacing favorites.txt, lastTitle.bin and the ISO title-ID cache.bin; readers can recover completed temporary last-title/cache records after interrupted replacement. 2026-10-15: partial parity. RiptOPL now first writes favourites.bin.new, retains that complete copy across an interrupted main-file rewrite, and recovers it on load (PR #945). It still cannot use an atomic rename on every memory-card filesystem; LUNA's separate last-title/cache staging details warrant independent comparison.Favorites staging
last-title staging

LU7Neutrino physical power-button DEV9-safe shutdown FixLUNA modifies Neutrino's IOP CD/DVD service so the native front-panel power event is handled by a priority-1 listener: it stops/synchronizes active emulated optical work, invokes DEV9's shutdown export when present, then issues the normal sceCdPowerOff. RiptOPL's OPL core already has its own orderly IGR/power-off path, but its Neutrino core does not include this coordinated DEV9 shutdown behavior.LUNA cdvdfsv.c
RiptOPL Neutrino baseline
LU8Neutrino in-game return / IGRLUNA extends the Neutrino runtime itself with a held L1+L2+R1+R2+START+SELECT IGR path. After 45 VBlank frames (roughly one second) it calls LUNA's shutdown RPC to quiesce optical/DEV9 activity, then uses the configured eec.ExitPath as the return ELF path, resets IOP and EE/GS state, and uses ExecPS2() to return directly to the memory-card ELF. RiptOPL has OPL-side IGR, but games launched through its current Neutrino core do not have Neutrino IGR, so this is a distinct missing runtime capability. The current README reports a physical FAT-PS2 test and requires installation on a memory card; this remains narrower than validation across all console and storage combinations.pad hook / combo

IOP shutdown RPC

LU11Neutrino VMC backing on APA/PFSMaps raw VMC files from an APA/PFS metadata partition into direct ATA extents for the Neutrino runtime. Requires normal boot, 8 KiB PFS zones, no indirect inode segments, and a shared 64-fragment budget for game plus both cards. The loader validates image geometry and mapping before launch. RiptOPL currently drops Neutrino VMC arguments for APA games because its bundled runtime lacks this PFS backing path. LUNA still requests a physical-console save/reboot/reload test; source implementation does not establish broad game compatibility.PFS extent mapping
Loader restrictions

Finishing a save before return remains the user's responsibility, and physical-console save safety is not established.LUNA build configuration · return routine · RiptOPL PS1 limitations
Current overlap and validation scope
Storage-source toggles, rescans, card creation/assignment, optional music and cheat selection are not counted again: RiptOPL already has those categories of functionality. LUNA's current README reports in-game return on a physical FAT PS2, while APA/PFS VMC save/reboot/reload and artwork performance on optional storage still need console testing. The older Constellation/Grid descriptions and HDD-only filter are superseded by the current frontend; neither describes today's selectable library views.

LU12PS1 disc-set workflows: two-to-four VCD images in ordered .m3u playlists, user-confirmed discovery, starting-disc picker and in-game swap in a matching LUNA runtime.Implementation · RiptOPL already lists PS1 VCD/Ember titles; this is separate disc handling. Gameplay proof pending.
LU13PS1 save-card lifecycle: shared/named pairs, import/export, two-card backups and interrupted-operation recovery.VMC management code. Do not relabel existing OPL/POPStarter VMC as absent.
LU14Console File Explorer: retained copy/move tray, verify-before-remove moves, rename/delete/create, text preview and ELF launching.File manager. Primarily a Launchstation parity concern, not evidence RiptOPL needs a file manager.

Overlapping source capabilities (now cross-listed as LU16–LU35): core selection between OPL and Neutrino, ordinary PS1/PS2 listings, basic PS2 VMC support, Favorites, artwork and selectable game sources. LU2–LU11 keep their prior evidence and qualified statuses; no old ID is silently reassigned.

Extended APA

L10N37/Open-PS2-Loader-Extended-APA — an OPL build for internal HDDs formatted with PS2 HDD Manager's banked APA layout.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
L1APA HDDs over 2 TiB Not plannedSplits a large internal HDD into APA “banks” and lists and launches games from every bank. RiptOPL stays on the standard APA layout — see Internal HDD.EXTENDED_APA.md

E2OPL

E2OPL — a historical OPL branch whose defining change was read-only EXT2 support for USB storage. Its published README requires the EXT2 partition to be first, uses 4096-byte blocks and supports ISO files rather than USBExtreme splitting.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
X1EXT2 USB filesystem supportLoads PS2 ISO images from a read-only EXT2 USB partition, avoiding FAT32's 4 GiB file limit and fragmentation requirement. RiptOPL supports FAT/exFAT-style BDM filesystems but has no EXT2 filesystem driver, so this remains a distinct storage-format capability even though exFAT solves most of the same practical problem today.SourceForge project
source README

belek666/Open-PS2-Loader modularPademu — a 2020 experimental branch that split PADEMU into controller-specific modules. DS3/DS4 overlap modern OPL, and its Xbox One USB path overlaps P1; the two controller classes below still have no equivalent module in RiptOPL.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
MP1Xbox 360 controllers over USB HistoricalA dedicated wired-Xbox-360 module recognizes Microsoft and several third-party VID/PID pairs, translates reports to DualShock 2 input and drives LEDs/rumble. RiptOPL has no xbox360usb PADEMU module.xbox360usb.c
MP2Generic USB HID gamepads HistoricalA generic HID module parses a USB gamepad's HID report descriptor and maps buttons, hats and axes into the emulated DS2 report instead of requiring a controller-specific driver. This is separate from Titan's keyboard/mouse support and BoliviaOS's USB keyboard support.hidusb.c
Modular PADEMU status

Stable Open PS2 Loader (sOPL)

mystyq/Stable-Open-PS2-Loader — a stability-focused fork. Its artwork TAR caching, large-list work, BDM HDD support, game-size fixes, MMCE work and other older changes either exist in RiptOPL or have since been superseded there. One lower-level ATA behavior remains materially different.

IDFeatureWhat it doesSource
S1ATA DRDY waiting for slow-starting SMR / SSD drives FixsOPL's in-game ATAD path adds an ata_wait_until_ready() loop that waits for BUSY to clear and DRDY to assert at driver start and around ATA commands. It was introduced specifically for newer SMR/SSD drives that can take longer to become ready. RiptOPL has its own post-ATAD settling and HDD-detection safeguards, but its current cdvdman/atad.c does not implement this in-driver DRDY wait strategy.commit 3495d55b
current atad.c
sOPL TAR claims vs. audited source
The May 2026 sOPL release text advertises ART/art.tar, CFG/cfg.tar and CHT/cht.tar. The published release tag points to an older commit, while current master visibly carries the ART reader/cache path. This survey does not turn an unlocated release-note claim into a separate gap. More importantly, RiptOPL rebuild/main now has a unified TAR engine for all three archive kinds, including art_cache.bin, cfg_cache.bin and cht_cache.bin, so none of those archive features is a current RiptOPL gap regardless.
Awaiting Comparison cleared
All eight variants that were previously reserved for comparison have now been audited. OPL Daily Build, Grimdoomer OPL and X2P produced no remaining unique RiptOPL gap and are recorded in Checked, nothing missing. The five sources above produced six confirmed entries.

Caduceus & Caduceus OPL versus PS2-Servers & RiptOPL

Verified Caduceus gaps: CA1–CA7 and CO1–CO7

14 source-specific entries, not 14 unrelated feature concepts. These include seven missing PC companion capabilities and seven console UI, integration or behavior differences. CO3 overlaps existing OR4; CO1 and CO7 are optional design choices; CO4 is experimental and not equivalent to a validated physical-console feature.

Audited code: The actual OPL snapshot was compared against RiptOPL code, and the Caduceus desktop modules against PS2-Servers. RiptOPL already has normal RA hashing, watch lists, SMB reconnect, PNG artwork support and an in-game unlock indicator; none is newly counted as missing. CO4 is richer presentation, not a missing RA notification system. CO6 is a more proactive check, not missing basic SMB recovery. Caduceus OPL's custom account protocol needs a companion endpoint and cannot be obtained just by swapping SMB servers.

Rian6/Caduceus is the Windows-side application (Electron/React/TypeScript/SQLite) that manages a game catalogue and launches the separately sourced OPLServer SMB server. Rian6/Caduceus OPL is its cooperating console-side OPL derivative. That is a tightly paired host + console loader, not one interchangeable executable. Their source repositories are distinct; the OPL source is also tracked as an opl-snapshot submodule.

Our comparable pairing is PS2-Servers (host-side network service) and RiptOPL (PS2 launcher). PS2-Servers deliberately serves multiple kinds of PS2 clients; Caduceus invests more heavily in a companion library and account experience. The table identifies host-side product gaps as well as console-side differences, without treating them as interchangeable RiptOPL feature gaps.

Keep protocol engines, headless portability, per-peer UDPFS handling and safety checks decoupled from any optional catalogue/RA frontend. Editions.

Compatibility and implementation boundaries

Do not assume the two ecosystems can be swapped by changing the server address. Caduceus OPL expects the paired PS2 SMB share (normally port 1024), plus custom RA catalogue/session and account APIs on UDP 18197/18198. PS2-Servers Desktop normally advertises SMBv1 share games on port 1025 (Core/Edge default to 1111). Share name, port, root layout, credentials, services and console configuration must match. Two services cannot both bind the same host address, protocol and port. Caduceus' custom achievement browser needs a compatible server component; serving the same ISO files via PS2-Servers does not supply it.

Both ecosystems inherit the upstream warning that RA telemetry during SMB game streaming can disrupt loads; neither pairing demonstrates a fix for that issue. Keep host tokens and the Caduceus read-capability key private; UDP catalogue traffic is not encrypted. Caduceus PC sources currently have no top-level declared GitHub repository licence, so obtain clear reuse permission or review licensing before copying original application/bridge code. The inherited OPL/xeRAbora components have their own licences and notices.

Integration priorities for our pairing

  1. Optional PC library module: add metadata/catalogue, cover preparation, installed-game inventory and non-destructive backup as a frontend/sidecar to PS2-Servers; keep existing service implementations unchanged. Account for RiptOPL's disc-ID PS2 art and filename/folder-based VCD, ELF and Ember art identities.
  2. Read-only game-session reporting: surface the running title and its image/cover to an optional desktop view; only add Discord presence if the session identity is dependable. Avoid injecting traffic or polling into the hot UDPFS/SMB transfer paths.
  3. Optional RA account viewer: build on the compatible xeRAbora client already used by RiptOPL, with secrets held on the host, bounded requests and a separately tested console-side browser. Do not introduce a competing telemetry client or incompatible protocol by default; OR4/CO3 are now implemented; use the RA guide for current browser support and validation limits.
  4. Integration tests first: verify share/port selection, art naming, concurrent listeners, offline fallback, IOP resets, real-PS2 UI memory limits and performance before treating the pairing as interchangeable. Preserve existing servers and saved libraries during any migration.

PS2CF — CompactFlash over the original PC CARD slot

mihhru/PS2CF 1.0 (2026-10-14; validation record) adds a different hardware storage bus, not merely another FAT32/exFAT formatting option. SCPH-10000 has hardware evidence; SCPH-15000 and SCPH-18000 are targets awaiting separate confirmation. CF/ATA adapter compatibility is hardware dependent. RiptOPL's implementation was merged in PR #955. CF1–CF3 therefore describe integrated capabilities awaiting real-console validation, not absent implementation. See the current CF guide. Related Launchstation file-manager work is tracked separately in #156; its status does not establish a RiptOPL hardware result.

IDFeatureGap and integration boundarySource
CF116-bit PC CARD CF/ATA hardware backendPS2CF detects a retail PCMCIA DEV9 revision (REV & 0xF0 == 0x20), checks controller/card state, reads CF CIS, selects a validated PIO profile and identifies ATA storage. RiptOPL now includes this distinct transport, with exclusive PC CARD ownership and runtime checks; hardware acceptance is pending. ROMVER/model must only be a hint; a PC CARD LAN adapter, empty slot, or owned DEV9 bus must never be seized.pccf_hw.c
CF2FAT32/exFAT CF game inventoryEnumerates PS2 ISOs from CF/SD-to-CF via the PC CARD slot, including single files larger than 4 GiB on exFAT. This is not identical to the already supported internal exFAT HDD or USB path. RiptOPL retains its own ART/CFG and image-title naming in the integrated backend.CF block-device registration · hardware/ISO examples
CF3Dedicated read-only in-game CF/CDVD driverThe menu and in-game IOP drivers are distinct (pccf_bd, pccf_game, pccf_cdvdman). CF4Companion wLaunchELF cf:/ file operations CompanionPS2CF's wLaunchELF adds CF browsing, copy, rename, create and delete on FAT32/exFAT. This is a Launchstation file-manager gap, not proof RiptOPL needs to become a file manager. Cross-project tracking: Launchstation #156. Direct ELF launch from cf:/ remains unsupported upstream.wLaunchELF CF IOP module

Integration safety: PS2CF disables competing network/HDD paths in its dedicated build. That blanket policy cannot be transplanted into RiptOPL. The new backend must own the PC CARD bus only when safe, fail closed on conflicts, preserve all other game devices, and not alter users' existing firmware or memory-card images. The current implementation reference defines ownership and remaining validation.

RIPPS2 — current collaboration deep dive

The detailed text in this earlier source section is retained as dated evidence. Use the full current refresh and current per-ID register above for updated pins, merged features and development status.

RIPPS2 collaboration review — October 25, 2026

Update, October 28: the build 119 audit, its port plan and the owner's decisions are in October 28 status. Where the two differ, that section is current.

RIPPS2 and RiptOPL share ancestry and exchange work. This review looks for useful mechanisms in both directions and preserves author credit. A difference is not automatically a defect, a finished feature or an instruction to port it.

Exact revisions and reconstruction

  • RiptOPL publishing source: 55261de1381ae70ecfdfb2054f9cafbcfcd586cf.
  • RIPPS2 main: e1e3504f4a8bfd6b2f88425d284eb845393cfcb9, Build 115 in progress.
  • Published v1.1.4-beta: f59abffde0794debdadaee526c175414a4b12b21, Build 114. GitHub marks this beta-named release non-prerelease; publication occurred October 24 in America/Los_Angeles (October 25 UTC).
  • b114: 20fcc576a2c01b172a095796f2b1bfa63c596238; no longer the sole place where Build 114 exists.
  • Imported RiptOPL upstream: 57c45b4fcc922ba00fed949c634caea0a2900c63, revision 4224. RiptOPL changes after that import must be reconciled separately.

The source was reconstructed from the previous pinned Build 113 tree and each exact numbered patch, with pinned test/asset files. No upstream shell build script was executed. Pins, changed-source comparisons, host checks and all tracked refs make the review reproducible.

What changed since the previous deep dive

Build 114 has shipped. Its master library, waiting rows, hidden games, cleanup planning, ISO/ZSO conversion, named color sweeps and smaller font configuration must now be described as released source rather than unreleased b114 work. Release status does not establish safe deletion, uninterrupted conversion or save durability on every backend.

Subsequent hotfixes add MMCE rename probing before conversion, remove RIPGRID sidebar strips and suppress background HDD error boxes. The MMCE firmware requirement and absence of a free-space check belong beside conversion claims, not buried in a changelog.

Build 115 implementation is present on main despite an older plan opening with “nothing built yet.” The numbered patch and testable functions carry more weight than that stale plan header. Main adds artwork aliases, runtime-aware Neutrino arguments, MMCE busy/wait handling, Trust Cache, input analog-mode retry, a Recent Games page and early-console PC Card/HDD UI consolidation. The plan's remaining proposals are not all delivered.

Wins worth carrying between the projects

Mechanism Why it helps Review before adoption
External Neutrino capability detection Avoid unsupported options causing an immediate return to Browser Unknown tags, strings/config heuristics, packed ELFs, ORBIT flags, explicit user arguments, aggregate argument budget
Artwork aliases and real filename lookup Accept OPL Manager artwork and mixed-case JPEG names without renaming libraries Alias precedence, cache invalidation, PFS case sensitivity, extra SMB/UDPFS misses, PS1 ID parsing
MMCE busy and game-card creation wait Avoid reading while firmware is copying/creating a card Firmware status contract, bounded wait/cancel, pad fairness, history writes before GameID, unrelated physical cards
Rename preflight Discover unsupported commit semantics before an expensive conversion Same-directory rename, unique scratch names, removal failure, firmware versions, no implied free-space guarantee
Master list / waiting rows / Trust Cache Present useful libraries before manually started drives answer Device replacement, duplicate serials, stale rows, memory limits, launch-time revalidation
Sidebar/grid focus ownership Predictable navigation and source changes Existing themes and controls, stale list snapshots, rendering cost, bounded selection
Selective fonts and single-pass HD Potentially reduce memory and rendering pressure Measure sections/VRAM and frame time; preserve CJK/custom TTF/CFF support and image quality
Recent Games page Recover recent choices across inactive sources Privacy/history policy, capped records, missing devices, per-core identity

The existing Build 113/114 deep dive retains the detailed source review of language hashes/RTL, sound conversion, screenshots, memory-card tools, write coordination and i.LINK. Its release/development labels are historical and are superseded above. Stable RI IDs remain in the current register.

Safety and compatibility boundaries

RIPPS2's Neutrino reset path rewrites mass-unit paths only when no -qb is emitted. This is not a universal path fix: -qb inherits a mounted environment and RiptOPL's local/network dispatch must preserve the intended device. A typed drive family is still not a unique physical device when two devices share that family.

Deletion, conversion and memory-card tools need separate designs for existing files, shared saves, interrupted writes, failed rename, media removal and recovery. A read-only APA preflight can reject malformed headers; it cannot prove that all real disks are safe to modify. Keep these as separate investigations rather than a bundled parity port.

The Build 115 hardware plan reports a problematic MMCE launch and incomplete automatically created GameID cards. It also reports a successful HDD launch. Those are upstream observations for the stated setup, not our tests. Its later Neutrino Burnout 3 result is a lab/emulator observation; it must not be described as an independent physical-console success. Earlier i.LINK results remain separated by native OPL, Ember, ordinary Neutrino, POPStarter and RA/ORBIT.

Shared wins and useful next experiments

RIPPS2 already imports RiptOPL fixes through its recorded upstream pin. YAOPL also explicitly credits fixes reviewed from RiptOPL. Shared code is acknowledged once rather than counted as a new competitive gap.

The first low-scope candidates are artwork interoperability and external-runtime option negotiation. For either, preserve the current loader/core contracts, make a small isolated change, run function-level host checks, then compare the same console/media/game against a control build. MMCE scheduling and storage-writing features need their own controlled work and hardware evidence. No implementation port is included in this documentation refresh.

No message was sent to another maintainer. These public, pinned findings provide a reviewable basis for sharing results when requested.

Scope of this focused 2026-10-15 pass: Source, patch files, build descriptions and stated hardware testing were compared where available. New CF/DB/RI/DL/OF identifiers are attributed to their sources; a gap is not a merge authorization or a claim of broad real-hardware compatibility. Before implementation, check latest RiptOPL and applicable PS2-Servers code for newly merged parity. Existing historical gap IDs and the 2026-10-08 broad audit are retained.

October 15 follow-up: desktop library, virtual SMB and visual OPL derivatives

A focused code comparison against RiptOPL and PS2-Servers identified five additional source-attributed differences. These are not five shipping commitments or five verified console improvements. The source snapshots and corresponding branches are pinned in Revision and validation scope. Previous Caduceus, OPLattice, RIPPS2, OPHTML and HTTP/UDPFS records remain separate; overlaps are called out below.

OrbitPS2 Manager (desktop)

Luden02/OrbitPS2-Manager is an Electron/Angular desktop library manager for Windows, macOS and Linux. It can import and catalog PS1/PS2 titles, download artwork, edit per-game CFG and manage VMC files. Those generic workflows overlap existing Caduceus CA entries and PS2-Servers' SQLite library/import tools; the two entries here deliberately track more specific operations.

IDDifferenceComparison and validationSource
OM1Bidirectional PS1 launcher-layout migrationOrbitPS2 imports CUE/BIN/ZIP to VCD and can convert an installed PS1 library between POPStarter-style XX./SB. launcher apps and direct POPS//VCD/ RiptOPL/POPSLoader layout, including a library-wide action. PS2-Servers has PS1 VCD catalog/hash support, but no equivalent confirmed bidirectional bulk launcher-layout conversion. Preserve artwork by VCD filename in any future integration; never overwrite source VCDs or remove a working launcher before a verified replacement exists.rename/convert service · PS1 import modes
OM2Batch ISO/ZSO file transformationLocal worker-pool ISO → block-indexed LZ4/ZSO conversion, batch compression and reverse per-file ZSO → ISO. This differs from playing or streaming compressed files (RiptOPL and PS2-Servers already do that). Generic queued imports and cover downloads overlap Caduceus CA2/CA3 or existing tools; the missing component is a desktop transformation workflow. Only import tested output, preserve originals by default and compare performance, filesystem limits and image integrity.ZSO worker-backed conversion

PS2SMB: HTTP range content presented as SMB files

forbenaj/ps2smb is a Python proof of concept: OPL still connects to a conventional SMB share, but the SMB server's file handles are backed by remote HTTP(S) ISO sources. Its source includes an Impacket SMB1/SMB2 virtual-file interception layer, a persistent chunk cache and a local admin/status endpoint. This is **not** RiptOPL's existing native HTTP game-streaming protocol and **not** ordinary local-file SMB hosting in PS2-Servers.

IDDifferenceScope, risk and statusSource
SM1Read-only virtual SMB share with HTTP-range sparse fetchingRemote file HEAD/Range probe, exact 206 Partial Content reads, 8-MiB disk-backed cache chunks, sequential prefetch and SMB1 file-open/read hooks. The PS2 sees a regular SMB game image; an HTTPS source must support reliable ranges. Upstream explicitly still requests a real-console game test; software tests do not establish consistent gameplay latency, FMV streaming or resets. This is a PS2-Servers host-side candidate, not a missing RiptOPL transport. Before adoption, enforce URL allowlists, bounded cache/storage, timeouts, content consistency and lawful user-supplied remote sources; avoid shipping arbitrary remote-catalog access by default.SMB hooks · HTTP range · persistent cache

Daniel's PS5-style Open PS2 Loader fork

danielnuld/Open-PS2-Loader extends the standard OPL theme element ABI; this is **not** evidence that RiptOPL lacks basic cover art, theme switching, Coverflow, per-game backgrounds or game-details screens. Unlike a configuration-only theme, the source implements new FrostedPanel, CardShelf, CoverWallpaper and ItemTitle primitives in the runtime. RiptOPL's current theme element vocabulary lacks those exact elements; porting the entire older fork would risk losing modern RiptOPL driver, library and achievements work.

IDDifferenceScope and evidenceSource
DN1GS framebuffer-based frosted-glass blur panel OptionalBlur render targets are sized for GS frame/texture stride alignment and composite a sampled backdrop behind a theme-controlled transparent panel. This is a discrete compositing operation, not simply a semitransparent rectangle. Require per-video-mode VRAM/GS fallback and profiling (480i/480p/other RiptOPL modes, music and background art active) before any hardware claim.GS blur render chain · FrostedPanel element
DN2CardShelf + animated CoverWallpaper + selected ItemTitle primitives OptionalHorizontal selection swaps navigation axes; shelf cards lift/scale the focused cover while crossfading wallpaper responds to the selection. ItemTitle exposes the readable game title separately from serial-ID ItemText. RiptOPL already implements alternative layouts, Coverflow and display-title logic, so this row counts only the distinct reusable theme-element API/presentation, not three missing game-library abilities. Front-end performance, texture lifetime and HD-video constraints need hardware validation.theme element implementation · theme configuration

Network-server ecosystem checked without new source-specific gaps

pcm720/udpfsd supports isolated concurrent UDPFS clients, read-only mode, UDPBD-with-UDPFS transport, Go binaries for embedded targets and optional CGO/libchdr CHD handling. YouKnow-sys/udpfs-server packages this family for Android. GorGylka/Server2PS2 provides an embedded Luckfox/Pico hardware deployment with SMB/UDPBD/UDPFS and a physical BOOT-button/LED mode selector. RiptOPL's PS2-Servers Desktop/Core/Edge already has UDPFS, UDPBD, SMB, embedded web UI, multi-client modes and portable deployment. None of those protocol categories is a newly missing RiptOPL game source; a device-specific button-controlled appliance is a different packaging/hardware proposition. Track as integration and server-protocol regression references rather than inflating feature counts. Edge does not presently include CHD or SMB2/3; Desktop/Core retain CHD where libchdr is installed. No end-to-end comparison on actual PS2 hardware was performed in this audit.

Audit boundary: this batch compared selected source modules, declarations, documentation and previous fork-gap evidence. The new entries are recorded as source-verified differences with explicit compatibility caveats. Other pre-existing IDs remain subject to the separate full re-audit described below.

What changed in this refresh

The detailed text in this earlier source section is retained as dated evidence. Use the full current refresh and current per-ID register above for updated pins, merged features and development status.

  • October 15 follow-up code comparison: added OM1–OM2 (OrbitPS2 Manager's PS1 launcher-layout conversion and batch ISO/ZSO transformation), SM1 (PS2SMB's HTTP-backed virtual SMB, with console validation pending), and DN1–DN2 (frosted GS blur and CardShelf/CoverWallpaper PS5 theme primitives). RiptOPL's existing HTTP, SMB, Coverflow, themes, ZSO playback and PS2-Servers' import tools are not gaps. Checked udpfsd, Android udpfs-server and Server2PS2 as supporting references without new ID assignments. Details and pinned source hashes are in this batch.
  • October 15, 2026 status reconciliation: OR3 (RA Neutrino), OR4/CO3 (console achievement browser), CO5 (paired Caduceus gating) and CO6 (SMB ECHO refresh) are implemented in RiptOPL and now marked as such in the searchable register. LU6 has improved Favorites staging but is not identical to LUNA's entire temp-file design. U9 is a closed, unmerged upstream experiment. These stable IDs remain in the historical ledger and source counts.
  • Focused October 15, 2026 source additions: added PS2CF (CF1–CF4; RiptOPL #955 / Launchstation #156), OPL-DualSense-BT (DB1–DB2), RIPPS2 (RI1–RI4), Enhanced PS2 Disc Launcher (DL1–DL2), and OPL FUTURE (OF1). Also recorded PS2-SNL, PS2BBL Extended and non-native launcher candidates without inflating the counted gaps. The previous source-wide audit remains dated October 8; this is a focused supplement, not a complete re-audit of every previously tracked repository.
  • Caduceus pair: the Windows-side library/RA companion and its paired OPL fork are now compared with PS2-Servers + RiptOPL. 14 CA/CO source-attributed entries were added after code inspection; CO3 overlaps OR4 and CO4 is richer experimental notification rendering, not an absent RiptOPL unlock overlay.
  • OPHTML: source is ahead of the v0.10.0 release. The reusable console-core archive is new OH12; per-game compatibility settings and last-played restoration overlap existing RiptOPL features. Planned cover decoding, search, Favorites and IGR are not counted as delivered features.
  • LUNA: the actual frontend/runtime gitlinks replace the old September snapshot. LU9/LU10/LU11 cover the new case-grid, experimental Scroll view and restricted APA/PFS Neutrino VMC implementation.
