New project comparisons — PSXCore, DS4RTBT and macOS tools

PSXCore (PX1–PX10) — POPStarter and Ember parity

Pinned revisions: LUNA@4614b0c5 still pins PSXCore@360a788f. Public PSXCore moved one commit to 66c70e9f (bounded unaligned upload-copy code), not yet integrated into LUNA. Since the last fork-gap PR update, LUNA itself changed README text only; this section audits features previously underclassified from its October 17–19 work.

Do not confuse runtime architectures. RiptOPL already launches PS1 VCDs via POPStarter, which uses Sony POPS. PSXCore likewise adapts the user-supplied Sony POPS executable in memory; it is not an independent emulator. Ember implements its own PS1 emulation from CUE/BIN data. Internal POPS patches cannot be transferred blindly to Ember, and without matched game/hardware tests a different source implementation is not proof of better compatibility.

IDPrecisely tracked source differenceExisting RiptOPL capability / limitations
PX1Optional decoded-code validation with a LUNA per-game control. Public tester defaults OFF.No matching PSXCore toggle in POPStarter/Ember UI. Test a known non-booting title before claiming improvement. Compatibility resolver.
PX2GPU-information query correction; texture-window wrapping; valid GPU DMA from PS1 RAM address zero; CLUT refresh; GTE register/colour fixes.Both existing cores already render PS1 graphics. These are granular correctness candidates rather than missing graphics support. Expected-byte patch references.
PX3Reusable, source-buildable, guarded POPS adapter and separate PS2 tester.An alternative to bundled POPStarter; generic PS1 launching is already delivered. Integration API.
PX4Optional third PS1 launch core, selectable per game and with a bounded ELF/bootstrap handoff.Frontend gap: RiptOPL's VCD rows use POPStarter and its CUE/BIN folders use Ember. PSXCore would require a distinct per-title choice, settings migration, preflight verification, fallback and device capabilities. Current two-core behavior.
PX5MDEC fast paths, RGB24 movie scanout, buffered interlace, raster and video-transition corrections.Ember and POPStarter already provide FMV playback and display modes; compare movie correctness and timing on specific discs. No cross-runtime test evidence yet. Source modules/flags.
PX6CD-ROM Play/SetMode audio-sector correction, SPU state preservation, capture IRQ, CDDA/reverb and PCM gap handling.Existing cores already play PS1 audio; this is a per-game audio fidelity investigation, not a missing sound system. CDDA/SPU patch · Reverb/MDEC modules.
PX7Guarded guest memory access, load/return delay behavior, scratchpad handling and loop-cycle scheduling.Compare CPU-sensitive games and representative scenes. Source changes in POPS are not a direct Ember implementation recipe. PSXCore correction summary.
PX8Checksum-verified POPS/runtime identity with bounded expected-byte, memory-layout and transactional patch checks.Safety candidate for a third core; don't assume undocumented POPStarter internals lack such safeguards. Transactional patch code.
PX9Native PS1 save-worker guards for paired-card readiness, precise writes, backup flush, rename and worker synchronization.POPStarter and Ember already save PS1 games. This is PSXCore's experimental write lifecycle, not a universal safe-save capability. The project admits no proven power-loss durability or save-aware IGR shutdown. Limitations.
PX10Two-to-four-disc identity validation: header/TOC hashes, exact sectors, consistent save binding and bounded disc paths.Complements LU12's LUNA playlist UI; more precise than filename-only grouping. Do not claim that the PSXCore tester can swap discs: it currently disables swap. Validator code.

What is already present versus genuinely new

FunctionRiptOPL POPStarterRiptOPL EmberLUNA / PSXCore
PS1 launchVCD on multiple local/remote backendsCUE/BIN game folders on supported backendsVCD runtime, LUNA-integrated option; standalone tester only USB and internal FAT32/exFAT
PS1 cardsExisting POPS/VMC and BDMA pathsAuto per-game cards and optional SharedMC.txt aliasesShared/per-game 128 KiB cards; LUNA named pairs, backup and restore; real-console persistence unproven
Sound / videoSony POPS audio, graphics and FMVsIndependent emulator with PAL timing, video modes, dither and audio optionsSpecific POPS audio/graphics corrections; no independent compatibility victory established
Disc setsPOPStarter-specific swap/reset methodsUpstream disc-file selection; no equivalent integrated IGR disc swap provenLUNA playlist grouping + separately enabled runtime swapping; standalone tester disables it
In-game returnPOPStarter's own reset semanticsNo Ember IGR in the RiptOPL docsLUNA PSXCore Quit/Play menu when compiled and installed for that path; public tester has it disabled

Experimental and incomplete capabilities (also inventoried)

Prepared native APA/PFS mapping is an experimental API and does not make an APA game browser/loader; RiptOPL's current POPStarter already has APA support. The published automatic compatibility profile catalog has zero entries. Adaptive Audio is OFF by default in the tester and may affect tempo/pitch. MX4SIO/MMCE and multiple USB support are not proven in the standalone tester. PSXCore's tester release has no established physical-console gameplay or save/reload validation, and IGR/disc swapping are disabled there, despite LUNA's separately configured runtime.

Validation and safe implementation scope

Preserve POPStarter and Ember as default choices. Any experimental PSXCore integration must verify user-provided POPS file checksums, patch bytes, ELF layout, per-title storage and memory-card ownership before IOP handoff. Compare identical legal disc data across all supported runtimes for startup, GPU/GTE effects, MDEC/FMVs, SPU/reverb/CDDA, controller behavior, disc swap, native saves after full reboot and IGR return. Label source presence, host-test success and actual hardware outcomes independently. No automatic migration, overwritten memory cards, blanket POPS patches, exclusive device claims or premature release.

OPL-DS4RTBT (DT1–DT3)

RTL-enabled Bluetooth fork adds Realtek firmware handling (DT1), DS4 clone HID output/CRC and rumble (DT2), and four-pad menu status plus controller setup/test (DT3). These extend DB1–DB2 (original DualSense BT/pairing), and are fully scoped in issue #972. Distinguish four menu pads from in-game multitap. Firmware licensing and real hardware remain verification gates.

PS2 Manager for macOS (MA1–MA2)

Native macOS manager supports PS2 ISO imports and PS1 CUE/BIN-to-VCD conversion with POPStarter multi-disc package sidecars (MA1), and per-game user-supplied POPS patch archives plus VCD reverse conversion (MA2). These are desktop-library workflow candidates for PS2-Servers and external utilities rather than missing OPL game-core functions.

Audit methodology and project coverage

“Fork gap” is a source-specific difference, not an invitation to copy every fork, or a guarantee that a feature works on every PS2. Each record should establish: (1) a named upstream revision or release; (2) a relevant implementation file or tested feature; (3) the corresponding RiptOPL behavior on a named branch; (4) whether the difference is an actual missing function, an optional design, a companion capability, an experimental claim, or already implemented; and (5) the evidence level: source / host tests / PCSX2 / real console.

Inclusive recording policy: Existing parity, UI alternatives, companion features, experimental flags, unsupported cases, bugs, withdrawn/historical work, and proposals remain documented and classified. Branding-only or empty forks can remain as directory references; they are not misrepresented as implemented code. Multiple sources may refer to one capability, and the record total is not a count of missing features. Mark delivered work as implemented instead of deleting it.

Reporting another project: link its repository or official source, exact branch/commit, feature code and hardware validation, then show where RiptOPL lacks (or already contains) it. Before creating another gap ID, check the register, the A–Z directory, and active PRs. Source differences affecting Launchstation, POPSLoader, launcHER or PS2-Servers should identify that companion rather than incorrectly claim an OPL game-loader gap.

Freshness: the October 8 comprehensive baseline has not been re-executed end-to-end. The October 15 pass corrects the five explicitly demonstrated implemented records, documents one partial-parity case and one closed historical proposal, and expands the project directory. The remaining records require revision-specific rechecks; their presence is not an assertion that they remain absent at HEAD.

Notes

2026-10-08 retired IDs: W4 (live disc-art toggle), W5 (pad tick-wrap fix) and H1 (pointer chains) are already implemented in RiptOPL, in src/themes.c, src/pad.c and ee_core/src/ra.c respectively. LU1 and LU3 describe removed LUNA views from the earlier snapshot; their IDs remain reserved. OR1/OR2 remain retired, with PS1 save-icon coverage now extended to VCD and Ember by PR #876.

  • OPHTML parity checked on 2026-09-27: OPHTML / ps2ui by coffeedevsolutions (Blake Coffee), audited against main@60810bd (version 0.10.0, .uib format v7). While publicly titled as a web-technology UI toolkit, the source contains an embedded OPL-compatible game launcher (ophtml.elf) and environment. Overlapping launcher features (Neutrino no-reset launch in src/elfldr_noreset.c, ISO9660 SYSTEM.CNF extraction in src/retrogem.c, texture streaming in src/texcache.c, and list windowing) were excluded as existing RiptOPL parity, leaving 11 genuine code-level gaps: declarative flexbox compilation to zero-allocation C99 GS runtime, pre-resolved pairwise HarfBuzz kerning on the EE, instant 1-byte palette theme switching, runtime 2D coordinate offsets, focus subtree visibility masking with automatic D-pad bypass, automated CRT overscan & multi-layer alpha contrast linter, browser-based theme simulation, static VRAM budgeting, Play! emulator CI testing, EE bus-alignment fuzzer hardening, and compiler safety ceilings.
  • LUNA parity checked on 2026-09-22: LUNA by darkladark, audited against main@23112ec and RiptOPL rebuild/main@abf14079. Eight source-backed gaps survived overlap checking. LU8 tracks LUNA's Neutrino in-game return because the implementation is present and RiptOPL's Neutrino core lacks IGR; it remains marked WIP because LUNA's current README has not promoted the feature to stable status.
  • Previously awaiting variants fully audited on 2026-09-22: E2OPL's historical SourceForge source; OPL Daily Build aa531a85; Modular PADEMU 66b93542; Grimdoomer's large-capacity branch d1f51be4 plus its later BDM LBA fix branch; historical uOPL 0daf81fe; X2P 160c3e18; exaconn master 73a4921d and negCon branch f9717f76; and Stable OPL 770d5323. Six new gaps survived code comparison; three projects moved to the no-gap table.
  • Project Titan Magenta Edition parity checked on 2026-09-22: audited against upstream main@2a69c259 / VERSION 0.3.93-rc17 and RiptOPL rebuild/main@abf14079. Titan's controller, Smart IGR and Store/Download documentation explicitly retains physical-target validation caveats; those rows describe source capability, not a hardware-compatibility guarantee.
  • NHDDL parity checked on 2026-09-20: RiptOPL already has the visual Game ID signal used by PixelFX/RetroGEM hardware, MMCE GameID switching for SD2PSX/MemCard PRO2 on cross-device launches, remembered last-played selection with optional autostart, persistent ISO metadata caching, and global/per-game Neutrino arguments. Those are therefore not N-series gaps.
  • Already in RiptOPL, so not listed: the fix from wOPL PR #435 (remember the last played game when it is the first list entry), PS2RD cheat images (.img), cover art from ART/art.tar (Interface settings), per-game configs from CFG/cfg.tar, cheats from CHT/cht.tar, persistent TAR index sidecars for all three archive kinds, the <name>_show_run theme key, RetroAchievements for physical discs, per-game OSD language, virtual memory card creation, and the older USB driver that upstream PR #1752 proposes pinning.
  • Shipped since the survey: Ratchet & Clank 3 over SMB (was U5, from upstream PR #1761), Pro Evolution Soccer 2 defaulting to Accurate Reads (was U7, see Compatibility Modes) and Galician and Serbian UI languages (was U8, see Languages), all in PR #658; remembered per-game cheat selections (was U2, from upstream PR #1748, in PR #846, see Cheat modes); Create VMC on First Launch (was OR2, in PR #842, see Game Launching); out-of-bounds reads checked against the real media size (was U3, from upstream PR #1763, in PR #840); in-game reset without the VBLANK interrupt (was U4, from upstream PR #1762, in PR #841, see In-Game Reset); and animated 3D save icons (was OR1, in PR #843, see Artwork Settings). IDs are not reused.
  • wOPL's config format: wOPL moved to libconfig. RiptOPL reads and writes both formats, so files the two loaders share, such as an app's title.cfg, keep working in both.
  • Device names: RiptOPL accepts usb0: and ata0: style names for its boot path and ties each launch to its device by driver name, but reads files through massN:. wOPL uses the typed names throughout. That is a different approach rather than a missing feature, so it is not counted.
  • TAR archive status: the older survey note about CFG/cfg.tar is obsolete. RiptOPL rebuild/main now has one indexed/persistently cached TAR engine for ART/art.tar, CFG/cfg.tar and CHT/cht.tar. Consequently, sOPL's TAR work is not a current gap, and an archive claim is counted only when its implementation can be located in the audited source.

Alphabetical PS2 loader and companion project directory

153 explicitly linked directory entries; every prior anchor retained. Loaders, companions, historical references and following forks are included; directory presence does not imply a confirmed gap or console test.

New comparison candidates (2026-10-15; no confirmed gap IDs): OSD-XMB merits priority source comparison for its plugin-driven PS1/PS2 library, on-device artwork downloads, Neutrino per-game settings, memory-card tools and device startup; XEB+ and its Neutrino plugin for dashboard/device/config workflows; MMCEMAN, OpenWRT UDPBD and RetroNAS for driver/server compatibility; and OPL Manager, Apollo Save Tool and HDL Batch Installer for PC/console library and save workflows. Boot exploits, optical-disc modification and external PC tools are included as references, not assumed OPL features. Verify code, existing RiptOPL parity, and physical-console safety before promoting any entry to a gap.

Assessment legend: “Tracked gaps” means one or more source-attributed register entries (some may have since shipped); “Reviewed / no counted gap” is a deliberate zero-gap finding; “Reference” is useful adjacent infrastructure; and “Further audit” means the project exists but no new difference has been demonstrated. How to contribute missing projects.

On this page

RiptOPL Documentation — for the opinionated Open PS2 Loader fork. Built on, and grateful to, the ps2homebrew team and the wider OPL family — see Credits & Sources. For the canonical project, see ps2homebrew/Open-PS2-Loader.

RiptOPL is a downstream fork, not the canonical project. POPSTARTER uses Sony's POPS; Ember is a separate emulator. Sony's POPS is not distributed here. PS1/POPSTARTER deep reference: the sister POPStarter docs.
